Let's design FFT gradient:
Inputs h array length n, w_p, w_q nonneg length n (w_p[0] excluded maybe). Define for P:
Wp_prefix[x] = ∑_{r=1}^{x} w_p[r] (prefix cumulative up to x; Wp_prefix[0]=0)
Wp_tail[x] = ∑_{r=1}^{n-1-x} w_p[r] = total_w - Wp_prefix[n-1-x]? Let cum[r]=∑_{s=1}^r w_p[s]; tail[x]=cum[n-1-x] if n-1-x>=1 else 0.
conv1_p[x] = ∑_{r=1}^{n-1-x} w_p[r] h[x+r]. This is cross-correlation of w_p (lag r) forward with h. Let array a=w_p (index r), b=h. We need for each x sum_{r} a[r] b[x+r]. This is correlation: if we reverse a, convolution. Define ar = a[::-1] length n. conv = np.convolve(ar, h, 'full')? (ar * h). Let's compute index. conv[k]=∑_i ar[i] h[k-i]. ar[i]=a[n-1-i]=w[n-1-i]. Let r=n-1-i => i=n-1-r. Then conv[k]=∑_{r=0}^{n-1} w[r] h[k-(n-1-r)] = ∑ w[r] h[k-n+1+r]. We want x -> h[x+r] with coefficient w[r]. If k = x+n-1, then conv[x+n-1]=∑ w[r] h[x+r] over valid r where x+r<n. Good. So conv1[x] = conv_w_h[x+n-1] where conv_w_h = np.convolve(w[::-1], h, mode='full') length 2n-1.
conv2_p[x] = ∑_{r=1}^x w[r] h[x-r]. This is standard convolution/ cross correlation: conv2 = np.convolve(w, h, 'full')? conv2[k]=∑_i w[i] h[k-i]. Set k=x: ∑_{r=0}^x w[r] h[x-r]. For r>0. Good. So conv2 = np.convolve(w, h, 'full')[0:n]. conv1 = np.convolve(w[::-1], h, 'full')[n-1 : n-1+n]? Check length. For x 0..n-1, index x+n-1. Then grad_p = Wp_tail - conv1 - conv2. Then add center term.
For q: grad_q = Wq_prefix - conv2_q - conv1_q.

Let's implement efficient binary swap local search:

Representation: n even, bits int8/float. We can maintain p and q arrays float/int length n (counts). Initial compute via autocorr and prefix exactly. For a swap i (1) ->0, j (0)->1, we need update p,q arrays. We can compute delta by applying two flips sequentially with vectorized slice updates. To evaluate without commit? We can copy p,q? For one move per iteration, we can apply directly and then revert if not accepted (or annealing). To decide acceptance, need new max. Applying two flips to p,q in-place O(n) vectorized; new max np.max O(n). If not accept, apply inverse? We can store old p,q? Better compute delta and check, then apply if accept.

Can we compute exactly delta vector for swap and quickly max without copying full? The changed p values for all r are old p + delta_p; delta arrays are piecewise: from flip i and j, each adds slices. L-BFGS objective:
```
def lbfgs_finish(h, n, alpha, mu, maxiter, deadline):
   def f(x):
       x=np.clip(x,0,1)
       val,_,_ = smooth_value(x,n,alpha) # maybe exact max? use soft
       val += 0.5*mu*(x.sum()-target)**2
       return val
   def g(x):
       x=np.clip(x,0,1)
       _, grad = smooth_grad(x,n,alpha)
       grad += mu*(x.sum()-target)
       return grad
   h_cur=h.copy()
   while time<deadline:
      res=minimize(f,h_cur,jac=g,method='L-BFGS-B',bounds,b={'maxiter':min(300,...)}...)
      h_proj=project_feasible(res.x,n)
      c=compute_c5(h_proj,n)
      if c<best: best...
      if budget bits break
```

`project_feasible(h,target)`: as previous but robust. Need if target not int? float.
```
def project_feasible(h, target):
 h=np.asarray(h,float).copy()
 h=np.clip(h,0,1)
 for _ in range(10000):
   diff=target-h.sum()
   if abs(diff)<1e-14: break
   if diff>0:
      room=1-h; s=room.sum()
      if s<1e-15: break
      h += diff/s*room
   else:
      s=h.sum()
      if s<1e-15: break
      h += diff/s*h
 h=np.clip(h,0,1)
 # exact correction
 diff=target-h.sum()
 if abs(diff)>1e-15:
    idx=np.where((h>1e-15)&(h<1-1e-15))[0]
    if idx.size:
       k=idx[np.argmin((abs(h[idx]-0.5)))?]
       h[k]+=diff
    else:
       # distribute to all? If all at bounds but diff small, adjust smallest if diff>0 else largest
       if diff>0: k=np.argmin(h); h[k]=min(1,h[k]+diff)
       else: k=np.argmax(h); h[k]=max(0,h[k]+diff)
 h=np.clip(h,0,1)
 return h
```
The iterative proportional adjustment can be overkill; but target same as initial sum. For small diff, one or two corrections enough. Use few iters to avoid time.

Implement stable soft weights:
```
def soft_max_weights(c, alpha):
 m=c.max()
 u=alpha*(c-m)
 u-=u.max()
 w=np.exp(u)
 s=w.sum()
 if s==0: w = c==m
 val=m + np.log(s)/alpha
 return val,w
```
Potential overflow if alpha*(c-m) negative large -> underflow ok; if positive max shifted 0. alpha up to 1e6, gaps small, okay.

`lbfgs_refine(h,n,alpha,mu,maxiter,deadline,best_c)`:
```
h_cur=h.copy(); target=n/2
bounds=[(0,1)]*n
state={'best':best_c,'best_h':h_cur}
def obj(x):
   x=np.clip(x,0,1)
   val,gradc,_ = ...? To avoid recompute in grad? okay.
   val *= 2/n
   val += 0.5*mu*(x.sum()-target)**2
   # maybe add tiny L2? no
   return val
def grad(x):
   x=np.clip(x,0,1)
   _,g = soft_grad(x,n,alpha)
   g *= 2/n
   g += mu*(x.sum()-target)
   return g
while time<deadline:
   res=minimize(obj,h_cur,jac=grad,method='L-BFGS-B',bounds=bounds,options={'maxiter':iter_left,'ftol':1e-20,'gtol':1e-12,'maxfun':...})
   h_cur=project_feasible(res.x, target)
   c=compute_c5(h_cur,n)
   if c<best: update
   if res.success and not drmin? break maybe
   if time ... break
   # If no improvement and diff sum small, break
return
```
Need `iter_left` perhaps 300 per call. Use outer schedule in run.

Because objective uses soft value not exact, may not monotonically reduce exact. We use exact for best.

Maybe use `alpha` scheduling and `mu`: small to large? For sum, after projection each batch, start h feasible. During batch, penalty keeps. Use mu=50. If target sum 250, dx=0.004. Gradient from C ~O(1)*dx? Actually g soft sums weights (sum1) times derivative each ~1, so g ~O(1); dx*g ~0.004. Penalty mu*diff: if diff 1e-2, mu=50 gives0.5, dominates; pushes sum quickly. At optimum after each project diff0. Good. If diff during iterations 0.1, penalty 5, strong. Fine.

Top-level `flip_delta(h,p,q,x,d)` modifies p,q in-place and assumes h[x] already set to new? We can design `apply_flip(h,p,q,x,newval)` that uses current h neighbors (with other swap already set) and updates h[x]. Implementation:
```
def apply_flip(h,p,q,x,newval):
    d = newval - h[x]
    if d == 0: return
    h[x] = newval
    n=len(h)
    if x < n-1:
       p[1:n-x] += d*(1.0 - h[x+1:n])
       q[1:n-x] -= d*h[x+1:n]
    if x > 0:
       p[1:x+1] -= d*h[x-1::-1]
       q[1:x+1] += d*(1.0 - h[x-1::-1])
```
For fractional, newval float. For binary, newval 0/1.


In objective, after computing x, if not finite return large. `mu` sum penalty.
```
def lbfgs_obj(x,n,alpha,mu):
  x=np.clip(x,0,1)
  val=soft_c5_value(x,n,alpha)
  diff=x.sum()-n/2
  val += 0.5*mu*diff*diff
  return val
def lbfgs_grad(x,n,alpha,mu):
  x=np.clip(x,0,1)
  val, g = soft_c5_grad(x,n,alpha) # returns grad in C units
  g += mu*(x.sum()-n/2)
  return g
```
`soft_c5_grad` returns grad of C (including dx). Good.

```
def active_fractional_descent(h,n,deadline,max_rounds=200):
    target=n/2
    h=project_feasible(h,target)
    p,q=compute_pq(h)
    cur=max(p.max(),q.max())
    best_h=h.copy(); best_c=cur
    # maybe p,q arrays are counts; C = cur*dx, but use count for comparisons
    for round in range(max_rounds):
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       # collect active lags
       cmax=p.max()
       active=[]
       for r in np.where(p >= cmax - max(1e-8,1e-5*cmax))[0]: active.append(('p',r,p[r]))
       for r in np.where(q >= cmax - max(1e-8,1e-5*cmax))[0]: if r>0: active.append(('q',r,q[r]))
       if p[0] >= cmax - tol: active.append(('p0',0,p[0]))
       # sort by val desc, unique (p0 only once)
       active.sort(key=lambda x: -x[2]) top 10
       best_pair=None; best_newcount=cur
       for typ,r,val in active[:8]:

           pos=np.where(g>1e-8)[0]; neg=np.where(g<-1e-8)[0]
           if not pos or not neg: continue

           neg=neg[np.argsort(g[neg])[:15]]
           # Evaluate pairs
           for i in pos:
             hi=h[i]; if hi<=1e-9: continue
             for j in neg:
                hj=h[j]; if hj>=1-1e-9: continue
                lim=min(hi,1-hj)
                if lim<=1e-8: continue
                # line search samples
                # skip if same i/j
                if i==j: continue
                for frac in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.75,1.0):

                    # optional skip tiny

                    if newc < best_newcount - 1e-12:

                # Maybe if best at frac=1, also try exact 1? included.
       if best_pair is not None:
           apply_transfer_inplace(h,p,q,best_pair[0],best_pair[1],best_pair[2])
           # recompute p,q to avoid drift
           p,q=compute_pq(h)
           cur=max(p.max(),q.max())
           if cur < best_c - 1e-12:
               best_c=cur; best_h=h.copy()

           g = smooth_active_grad(h,n) maybe; evaluate top few; if no break
           break
    h=project_feasible(h,n/2)
    c=compute_c5_exact(h,n)
    if c < best_c*d? return h,c_best? We'll return best_h and its exact c.
    return best_h, compute_c5_exact(best_h,n)
```

But `best_c` stored as count, not C. Better store count `cur_count`, final multiply. For comparisons, use count. In active descent, `transfer_new_max` returns count. At end, convert.

`active_gradient` top-level:
```
def active_p_gradient(r,h,n):
  g=np.zeros(n)
  m=n-r
  if m<=0: return g
  g[0:m] += 1 - h[r:]
  g[r:] -= h[0:m]
  return g
def active_q_gradient(r,h,n):
  g=np.zeros(n)
  m=n-r
  if m<=0: return g
  g[r:] += 1 - h[0:m]
  g[0:m] -= h[r:]
  return g
def active_p0_gradient(h): return 1-2*h
```
These derivative wrt counts (not multiplied by dx). Fine.

`transfer_new_max` top-level uses `apply_flip` on copies. It should compute p,q after two flips exactly. Need p,q passed are current. For fractional transfer, h old. Implementation:
```
def transfer_new_max(h,p,q,i,j,t):
    ht=h.copy(); pt=p.copy(); qt=q.copy()
    a=ht[i]; b=ht[j]
    # ensure t limited
    t=min(t, a, 1-b)
    if t <=0: return max(pt.max(),qt.max())
    apply_flip(ht,pt,qt,i,a-t)
    apply_flip(ht,pt,qt,j,b+t)
    return float(max(pt.max(),qt.max()))
```
But `apply_flip` uses `newval - ht[x]`; for i, ht[i]=a; ok. For j, after first ht[j]=b still; ok. It sets ht[j]=b+t. Works.

A flip changes p0 by: old h(1-h), new. Let's implement softmax L-BFGS for K<=12. Need top-level obj/grad:
```
def basis_obj_grad(y, B, n, alpha, mu_sum? no) returns soft C * dx.
   a=softmax(y)
   h=(a[:,None]*B).sum(0)
   val, ggrad = soft_c5_grad(h,n,alpha)
   # ggrad wrt h
   bh = B.T # K x n
   proj = bh @ ggrad  # K
   hproj = np.dot(ggrad, h)
   grad_y = a * (proj - hproj)
   return val, grad_y
```
Since ∂h/∂y_s = a_s(b_s - h). Good. No sum penalty needed because softmax ensures sum target if all basis sum target. Ensure basis sum target within tolerance. Values in [0,1]. For `h` from convex combo, sum = a·(B.sum(axis=1)) = target. Good.

Use `minimize` L-BFGS-B on y (unbounded). Bounds none. Maxiter maybe 300. Objective uses soft C; final evaluate exact. Multiple starts: uniform; one-hot for best basis; random. But if too many basis, K=10.

Inside binary_local_search, score_interval. We need update top lists recomputed after many steps. Since h changes, ZR/ZB update. Implementation details:
```
def binary_local_search(h0,n,deadline,rng,max_iter=200000):
    target=n//2
    h=binarize(h0,target)
    p,q=compute_pq(h)
    cur=count_max(p,q)
    best_c=cur; best_h=h.copy()
    ones=list(np.where(h>0.5)[0]); zeros=...
    temp=0.2
    no_improve=0
    score_interval=20
    evals_per_iter=4
    it=0
    while it<max_iter and time.time()<deadline:
       if it % score_interval ==0:
          maxp=p.max(); maxq=q.max()
          ZR,ZB=zero_counts(h,n)
          if maxp>=maxq:
             score_one=ZR - 0.3*ZB
             score_zero=ZR + 0.3*ZB
          else:
             score_one=ZB - 0.3*ZR
             score_zero=ZR + 0.3*ZB
          top_ones=np.array(ones)[np.argsort(-score_one[np.array(ones)])][:25]
          top_zeros=np.array(zeros)[np.argsort(score_zero[np.array(zeros)])][:25]
          if len(top_ones)==0 or len(top_zeros)==0: break
       # Generate candidates: loop e to evals
       best_new=cur+1; best_pair=None
       if no_improve < 50:
          ie=0
          while ie<evals_per_iter and len(top_ones)>0 and len(top_zeros)>0:
             i=top_ones[ie % len(top_ones)]
             j=top_zeros[(ie*7) % len(top_zeros)] # diverse
             # maybe also random from top
             nm=swap_new_max(h,p,q,int(i),int(j))
             if nm < best_new: best_new=nm; best_pair=(i,j)
             ie+=1
       else:
          # random candidates plus perturbation
          for _ in range(evals_per_iter):
             i=ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]; j=zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]
             nm=swap_new_max(h,p,q,i,j)
             if nm<best_new: ...
       if best_pair is not None:
          # accept? if best_new <= cur or anneal
          d=best_new-cur
          accept = best_new < cur - 1e-12 or (d>0 and rng.random() < np.exp(-d/temp))
          if accept:
             apply_swap_inplace(h,p,q,best_pair[0],best_pair[1])
             # update lists
             i,j=best_pair
             ones.remove(i); ones.append(j); zeros.remove(j); zeros.append(i)
             cur=best_new
             if cur < best_c:
                best_c=cur; best_h=h.copy()
                no_improve=0
             else:
                no_improve += 0? # accepted worse, reset? no
             if accepted worse maybe no_improve? We'll just not count.
          else:
             no_improve += 1
       else:
          no_improve += 1
       it +=1
       temp = max(0.02, temp*0.9998)
       if no_improve > 1000:
          # perturb: do random swaps 3-10 regardless
          for _ in range(rng.randint(3,10)):
             i=ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]; j=zeros[...]
             apply_swap_inplace; update lists
          cur=max(p.max(),q.max())
          no_improve=0
          # recompute scores next
       if cur < best_c: best...
    return best_h, best_c*dx? We can return count or C.
```
Need top_ones as numpy int arrays; ones/zeros Python lists. For random, if lists empty break.

`swap_new_max` copies h,p,q. For binary, h copy; apply_swap_inplace uses apply_flip; okay. This is O(n) memory per eval. For 4 evals/iter. If 100k iters, 400k*3*500 elements=600M, maybe okay but Python slice overhead. Could be high. Use evals_per_iter=2 or adaptive based on time. Maybe set `evals_per_iter = 3`.

We can optimize `compute_pq` using FFT for autocorr to O(n log n). Since we need p/q often. Let's implement FFT autocorr for speed and to support n up to 2000. `np.correlate(h,h,'full')` direct in C but O(n^2). For n=1000, 1M multiply, okay; for many calls, FFT maybe faster. Use `scipy.fft.fft`? Allowed scipy. Implement `autocorr(n, h)` using `fftconvolve(h, h[::-1])`. Let's derive.
Autocorr A_r = ∑_{i=0}^{n-1-r} h_i h_{i+r}. This is cross-correlation. Using convolution: `conv = fftconvolve(h, h[::-1], mode='full')` length 2n-1. What indices? Let a=h, b=h_rev (b_i = h_{n-1-i}). conv[k]=∑ a[k-i] b[i]. Want A_r? Another way: full correlation `np.correlate(h,h)` can be computed via FFT: f=fft(h, m); c=ifft(f*conj(f)).real; c = [A_{n-1},...,A0,A1,...]? For unnormalized, `c = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(h, m) * np.conj(...)).real`. Index m-1 corresponds A0. This might be faster. Implement with numpy only:
```
def autocorr_full(h):
   n=len(h)
   m=1
   while m < 2*n-1: m <<=1
   f=np.fft.rfft(h,m)
   ac=np.fft.irfft(f*np.conj(f), m)[:2*n-1]
   # ac[k] = ∑ h_i h_{i-k}? Let's verify. For real, irfft of |F|^2 gives circular autocorrelation: r[l]=∑ x_i x_{i+l mod m}. For l=0 sum squares. For positive l, ∑ x_i x_{i+l}. This is not linear with zero padding? Because padding zeros, for l<n it's A_l? r[l] = ∑_{i=0}^{m-1} x_i x_{i+l mod m}. With zeros padding, for l=1..n-1, i=n-l..m-1 zero? term x_i x_{i+l} mod m includes wrap to x_{i+l-(m)}; since m>=2n-1, for i near end and l, wrap index >? x beyond n zero, but modulo could wrap to beginning? Wait circular length m: if i+l >= m, wraps. Since m>=2n-1, i up to n-1, l up to n-1, i+l <=2n-2 < m, no wrap. So r[l]=∑_{i=0}^{n-1-l} h_i h_{i+l}=A_l for l=0..n-1. For negative l, r[m-l]=A_l. Good.
   return ac # length m, ac[1:n] = A1..A_{n-1}? ac[0]=A0.
```
Thus we can get A0=ac[0], A=ac[1:n] **[A1..A_{n-1}]**. But our previous A array needed A_r for r=0..n-1. We can use FFT ac directly: `A = np.zeros(n); A[0]=ac[0]; A[1:]=ac[1:n]`. Wait ac[1] = A1, yes. That's faster and aligns no reversal. Let's test h=[1,0,0], n=3. m=8. ac[0]=1, ac[1]=0, ac[2]=0. Good. For [0,1,0], A0=1,A1=0,A2=0. Good. For h=[1,1,0], A0=2,A1=∑h_i h_{i+1}=1*1+1*0=1,A2=0. FFT ac[1]=1. Good.
Then `P_r = S[n-r]`, qbase. p=P-A, q=S[n]-S[r]-A. A vector length n with A[0]=A0, A[1:]=A1... Good. This avoids correction alignment. Need ensure FFT numerical small imaginary; real. For very small n (e.g., n<32), direct np.correlate maybe faster/accurate. But okay all. The FFT uses m power of two; for n=500,m=1024; cheap. For n=1? m=1? handle.
Let's implement `compute_pq_fft(h)`:
```
n=len(h)
if n <= 64:
   aut=np.correlate(h,h,'full'); A=aut[:n][::-1]
else:
   m=1
   while m < 2*n-1: m <<=1
   f=np.fft.rfft(h,m)
   ac=np.fft.irfft(f*f.conj(), m)[:2*n-1]
   A=np.empty(n); A[0]=ac[0]; A[1:]=ac[1:n]
   # ac length at least n
S=np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(h)))
P=S[n - np.arange(n)]
Qbase=S[-1] - S[:-? r] -> S[n]-S[0:n]
p=P-A
q=S[-1]-S[:-1?] - A
return p,q
```
`S[n - np.arange(n)]` for n=500 okay. `S[:-?]`: `S_r` for r=0..n-1 is `S[:n]` (length n). So qbase = S[-1] - S[:n]. Good.

This will speed p/q computations dramatically for n>64. Great.

Now, gradient conv can also use FFT via fast_conv. For n=500, FFT maybe faster. Use `fast_conv` threshold maybe 100000 to use FFT? But FFT has overhead and small errors.
```
def fast_conv(a,b):
   if len(a)*len(b)<100000: return np.convolve(a,b,'full')
   nconv=len(a)+len(b)-1
   m=1
   while m<nconv: m*=8? power2
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,2*m?) Actually m>=nconv.
   fb=np.fft.rfft(b,m)
   out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,m)[:nconv]
   return out
```
Use own to avoid scipy.signal import? SciPy allowed. Numpy FFT okay. But rfft length m power of two. For m maybe 1024. Good.
Implement:
```
def fast_conv(a,b):
    la,lb=len(a),len(b)
    if la==0 or lb==0: return np.array([])
    nconv=la+lb-1
    if la*lb <= 200000:
        return np.convolve(a,b,'full')
    m=1
    while m<nconv: m <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,m)
    fb=np.fft.rfft(b,m)
    out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,m).real
    return out[:nconv]
```
Direct threshold 200k; for n=500 length product 250k -> FFT. FFT conv returns small negative. Gradient okay.

`soft_c5_grad` uses 4 FFT convs plus p/q FFT. For n=500, m=1024, 5 FFTs ~ very fast. This allows more L-BFGS iterations.

Let's test gradient formula with FFT conv orientation again. For wp_full [w0=0,w1...], `fast_conv(wpw[::-1], h)`. With direct, we tested. FFT should match.

Let's write `apply_flip` carefully with p0 update and slices. Need when x=0, `h[x-1::-1]` empty? Use if x>0. When x=n-1, slice `1:n-x` -> `1:1` empty. Good.
```
def apply_flip(h,p,q,x,newval):
    old = h[x]
    d = newval - old
    if d == 0: return
    p[0] += d*(1.0 - old - newval)
    q[0] += d*(1.0 - old - newval)
    h[x] = newval
    n=len(h)
    if x < n-1:
       p[1:n-x] += d*(1.0 - h[x+1:n])
       q[1:n-x] -= d*h[x+1:n]
    if x > 0:
       p[1:x+1] -= d*h[x-1::-1]
       q[1:x+1] += d*(1.0 - h[x-1::-1])
```
Check p0 delta formula: old=0.5,new=0.4,d=-0.1: delta = -0.1*(1 -0.9)= -0.001; actual 0.24-0.25=-0.01? Wait 0.5*(0.5)=0.25; 0.4*0.6=0.24; delta -0.01. Formula d*(1-old-new)= -0.1*(1-0.5-0.4)=-0.01 correct. Good.
For binary old1,new0: -1*(1-1) =0. old0,new1:1*(1-1)=0.

`apply_swap_inplace`: If h not binary, transfer? For binary swap, use new vals 0,1. If h[i] maybe not exactly 1? In binary local, yes. For safe:
```
def apply_swap_inplace(h,p,q,i,j):
    apply_flip(h,p,q,i,0.0)
    apply_flip(h,p,q,j,1.0)
```
If h[i] old 1, h[j] old0. If not, not use.

`swap_new_max` copies and applies. If h binary, new max count. If h continuous? not use.

`transfer_inplace`:
```
def apply_transfer_inplace(h,p,q,i,j,t):
   t=float(t)
   a=h[i]; b=h[j]
   t=min(t,a,1.0-b)
   if t<=0: return
   apply_flip(h,p,q,i,a-t)
   apply_flip(h,p,q,j,b+t)
```
Good.

`transfer_new_max`: same on copies. Need `pt,qt` copies; `ht` copy. After applying, max. This modifies ht. Good.

In active descent, line search samples fractions. To avoid too many, for each pair maybe sample only 4 fractions: [0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0] plus if derivative small [0.05]. We can do 6.
Potential issue: `transfer_new_max` uses p,q from current. For each pair, copies O(n). For 64 pairs*6=384 copies per active, 10 active=3840 copies/round; n=500 => 2M elements, okay for few rounds. But if many rounds 100, 200M, okay. Slice ops 3840*2 flips*4 slices=30k slice ops per round, maybe heavy. Limit active lags 5 and pairs 8x8=64, samples 5 =>2560 eval/round. Rounds maybe 30 ->76k eval, okay.

Top positions for gradient: If gradient all zero, skip. For p0, pos where h<0.5? grad=1-2h: positive if h<0.5. For transfer, we want decrease i with grad>0 and increase j grad<0. Good. However for p0, decreasing i and increasing j both reduce p0? p0 derivative 1-2h; If i low (h<0.5) grad positive? decreasing low h? If h=0.2, derivative 0.6 positive; decreasing h reduces p0? p0 term h(1-h), derivative positive, yes decreasing reduces. Increasing j with h=0.8 grad -0.6, increasing reduces. So transfer from low to high reduces p0 (makes values more extreme), interesting. For p_lag, similar.

Pair candidate line search: If `i` and `j` order doesn't matter; transfer decreases i, increases j. For p_lag gradient positive at i, negative at j. Good.

Now, binary local search top-level detailed code. Need avoid `np.array(ones)` many times. Use score interval and keep arrays `ones_arr`, `zeros_arr` updated? Updating after swap with list and array mismatch. Simpler: maintain Python lists; at score interval, create arrays. For random candidate, list indexing O(1). For top_ones, store list of ints from sorted top. Update after accepted? Top lists stale for 20 iterations, okay.
Pseudo:
```
def binary_local_search(h0,n,deadline,rng,quick=False):
    target=n//2
    h=binarize(h0,float(target))
    p,q=compute_pq(h)
    cur=float(max(p.max(),q.max()))
    best_c=cur; best_h=h.copy()
    ones=list(np.nonzero(h>0.5)[0]); zeros=list(np.nonzero(h<0.5)[0])
    temp=0.25
    no_improve=0
    score_interval=15
    evals=3 if quick else 4
    it=0
    max_iters=500000
    top_ones=[0]; top_zeros=[1]
    while it<max_iters and time.time()<deadline and ones and zeros:
       if it % score_interval ==0:
          maxp=float(p.max()); maxq=float(q.max())
          ZR,ZB=zero_counts(h,n)
          ones_arr=np.array(ones,dtype=int); zeros_arr=np.array(zeros,dtype=int)
          if ones_arr.size==0 or zeros_arr.size==0: break
          so = ZR[ones_arr] - 0.35*ZB[ones_arr]
          sz = ZR[zeros_arr] + 0.35*ZB[zeros_arr]
          if maxp < maxq:
             so = ZB[ones_arr] - 0.35*ZR[ones_arr]
          # if zeros score, for q-heavy maybe ZB +0.35ZR
          if maxp < maxq:
             sz = ZB[zeros_arr] + 0.35*ZR[zeros_arr]
          top_ones = ones_arr[np.argsort(-so)[:25]].tolist()
          top_zeros = zeros_arr[np.argsort(sz)[:25]].tolist()
          if not top_ones or not top_zeros: break
       best_nm=cur+1.0; best_i=-1; best_j=-1
       # try top cyclic candidates
       for e in range(evals):
          i=top_ones[(e*3) % len(top_ones)]
          j=top_zeros[(e*5+1) % len(top_zeros)]
          if i==j: continue
          nm=swap_new_max(h,p,q,int(i),int(j))
          if nm < best_nm:
             best_nm=nm; best_i=int(i); best_j=int(j)
       # occasional random if no_improve high
       if no_improve > 200 and evals>0:
          for _ in range(2):
             i=ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]; j=zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]
             nm=swap_new_max(...)
             if nm<best_nm: ...
       if best_i>=0:
          d=best_nm-cur
          accept = (d < -1e-12) or (d > 0 and rng.random() < np.exp(-d/temp))
          if accept:
             apply_swap_inplace(h,p,q,best_i,best_j)
             # update lists
             try: ones.remove(best_i)
             except ValueError: pass
             try: zeros.remove(best_j)
             except ValueError: pass
             zeros.append(best_i); ones.append(best_j)
             cur=best_nm
             if cur < best_c - 1e-12:
                best_c=cur; best_h=h.copy(); no_improve=0
             else:
                no_improve += 1
          else:
             no_improve += 1
       else:
          no_improve += 1
       it +=1
       temp = max(0.02, temp*0.99985)
       if no_improve > 800:
          # kick random swaps
          k=rng.randint(3,12)
          for _ in range(k):
             if not ones or not zeros: break
             i=ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]; j=zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]
             apply_swap_inplace(h,p,q,i,j)
             ones.remove(i); zeros.remove(j); zeros.append(i); ones.append(j)
          # recompute p/q to ensure
          p,q=compute_pq(h); cur=float(max(p.max(),q.max()))
          no_improve=0
          # also update best? if cur<best
          if cur<best_c: best_c=cur; best_h=h.copy()
    dx=2.0/n
    return best_h, float(best_c*dx)
```
Need note: In accept worse with annealing, we update cur=best_nm even if >best; no_improve? Could reset to allow exploration. We'll set `no_improve=0` on any accept, else increment.
Potential issue: `p,q` after accepted swap exact; after kick recompute. Good.
`zero_counts` uses h as float 0/1; okay.

Time: score_interval 15, each interval compute ZR/ZB O(n). Good.

```
def make_starts(n, parent, rng):
  starts=[]
  if parent is not None and len(parent)==n: starts.append(parent)
  # ensure all length n float
  h=thue_morse(n); starts.append(h)
  starts.append(random_balanced(n,rng))
  starts.append(random_balanced(n,rng))
  for alpha in [0.6180339887, 0.7071, 0.6667, 0.5556]:
      starts.append(beatty_binary(n,alpha,rng.uniform(0,1),rng))
  starts.append(wave_binary(n,1.0,0.0,rng))
  starts.append(wave_binary(n,2.0,1.0,rng))
  starts.append(wave_binary(n,3.0,2.0,rng))
  # maybe parent binarized
  if parent is not None:
      starts.append(binarize(parent,n/2))
  # deduplicate? Evaluate later.
  return starts
```
Need `thue_morse` returns float 0/1 but density maybe not exact; binarize to target m:
```
def thue_morse(n):
   arr=np.fromiter(((i.bit_count() & 1) for i in range(n)), dtype=float)
   return binarize(arr, n/2)
```

In `basis_refine`, if K=1 skip.
```
def basis_refine(cands,n,deadline,best_c,best_h):
   B_list=[]
   for h in cands:
      if h is None: continue
      h=project_feasible(np.asarray(h,float),n/2)
      B_list.append(h)
   if not B_list: return best
   # remove almost duplicates: compare max abs diff <1e-6 skip
   uniq=[]
   for h in B_list:
       if all(np.max(np.abs(h-u))<1e-5 for u in uniq): continue
       uniq.append(h)
   if len(uniq)>12: uniq=uniq[:12]
   B=np.array(uniq)
   # starts y
   starts=[np.zeros(K)]
   for k in range(min(K,5)):
      y=np.full(K,-8.0); y[k]=8.0; starts.append(y)
   for _ in range(3):
      starts.append(rng.normal(size=K)*2)
   for y0 in starts:
      if time>deadline: break
      # L-BFGS with alpha 10000, maxiter=200
      res=minimize(basis_obj, y0, jac=basis_grad, args=(B,n,20000), method='L-BFGS-B', options={...})
      a=softmax(res.x); h=B@a; c=compute_c5_exact
      if c<best: update
      # coordinate descent on alpha
      h,a cur count
      for rd in range(50): if time>deadline: break
         improved=False
         for s in range(K):
           for t in range(K):
             if s==t or a[s]<=1e-6: continue
             for frac in [0.1,0.3,0.5,0.7,1.0]:
                a2=a.copy(); delta=a[s]*frac; a2[s]-=delta; a2[t]+=delta
                if a2[s]<-1e-12: continue
                a2=np.clip(a2,0,1); a2/=a2.sum()
                h2=B@a2; c2=compute_c5_count(h2,n)*dx
                if c2<cur-1e-12:
                   a=a2; h=h2; cur=c2; improved=True; break
             if improved: break
           if improved: break
         if not improved: break
         if cur<best: update
   return best
```
Need `basis_obj` and `basis_grad` top-level. They use soft_c5_grad count; multiply dx in obj.
```
def basis_obj(y,B,n,alpha):
   a=softmax(y); h=B@a; val,g=soft_c5_grad(h,n,alpha); return (2/n)*val
def basis_grad(y,B,n,alpha):
   a=softmax(y); h=B@a; val,g=soft_c5_grad(h,n,alpha)
   proj=B.T@g; hproj=float(np.dot(g,h))
   return (2/n)*a*(proj - hproj)
```
No penalty. Sum of B columns target ensures h sum. But if B rows have small sum errors, h sum error; could add no penalty. We project B rows so fine.

Coordinate descent uses `compute_c5_count` maybe p/q; exact enough. Use dx.

```
def fourier_refine(n,deadline,rng,h_seed):
   K=4
   X=fourier_features(n,K)
   L=X.shape[1]
   best_h=None; best_c=inf
   cands=[]
   if h_seed is not None:
       try: cands.append(np.linalg.lstsq(X, h_seed, rcond=None)[0])
       except: pass
   cands.append(np.zeros(L))
   for _ in range(3): cands.append(rng.normal(size=L)*1.5)
   # maybe large coefficients to sharpen: c[1:]=rng.choice([-3,3])*...
   for c0 in cands:
       for alpha in [2000,10000]:
          if time>deadline: break
          bounds=[(-12,12)]*L? L-BFGS-B bounds to avoid huge. Use bounds to keep sigmoid not too flat.
          res=minimize(fourier_obj, c0, jac=fourier_grad, args=(X,n,alpha,20), method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter':200})
          h=sigmoid_features(c0? res.x, X)
          h=project_feasible(h,n/2); c=compute_c5_exact
          if c<best: update
   return best_h,best_c
```
`fourier_features`: use frequencies 1..K over [0,2]: `x = np.linspace(0,2,n,endpoint=False)`. Columns: const 1, for m: cos(π*m*x), sin(π*m*x). Period 2? cosπm x has m half cycles over length2? Frequency m/(2)? okay.
`fourier_h(c,X)`:
```
f=X@c; f=np.clip(f,-12,12); h=1/(1+np.exp(-f))
```
But derivative of clipped f: if using generate h via clip, gradient formula not exact at clip. To keep gradient exact, don't clip f except exp safe: `np.exp(-f)` with f large may under/overflow. Use `f=np.clip(f,-50,50)`, derivative discontinuity only beyond 50; bounds 12 avoid. We'll set bounds ±10, derivative fine.
Bounds ±10 for all coefficients. Good.

`fourier_obj`:

```
h=fourier_h(X@? c,X); val,g=soft_c5_grad(h,n,alpha); val*=2/n; diff=h.sum()-n/2; val +=0.5*mu*diff^2; return val
```
`fourier_grad`: as above plus penalty. `g_h` count grad; scale dx.

```
def resample_h(h,n):
  x_old=np.linspace(0,2,len(h),endpoint=False)
  x_new=np.linspace(0,2,n,endpoint=False)
  return np.interp(x_new,x_old,h)
```

Implement soft weights guard:
```
def soft_max_weights(c, alpha):
   c=np.asarray(c,float)
   m=float(c.max())
   if not np.isfinite(m): return m, np.full_like(c,0.0)
   if alpha <=0:
      w=(c==m).astype(float); w/=w.sum(); return m,w
   u=alpha*(c-m)
   u-=u.max()
   w=np.exp(u)
   s=w.sum()
   if s<=0 or not finite: w=(c==m).astype(float); w[w>0]/=w.sum(); return m,w
   w/=s
   val=m+np.log(s)/alpha
   return val,w
```
If alpha huge (1e6) and c differences ~1e-14, u small. okay.

`compute_pq` with FFT: Need if h length 1, p=q=[h(1-h)]; full? n=1, dx=2, not relevant. Ensure code handles.
```
def compute_pq(h):
  n=len(h)
  if n==0: return np.zeros(0),np.zeros(0)
  if n==1:
    p=np.array([h[0]*(1-h[0])]); q=p.copy(); return p,q
  if n <= 64:
     aut=np.correlate(h,h,'full')
     A=aut[:n][::-1].astype(float)
  else:
     m=1
     while m < 2*n-1: m <<=1
     f=np.fft.rfft(h,m)
     ac=np.fft.irfft(f*np.conj(f), m)[:2*n-1]
     A=np.empty(n)
     A[0]=ac[0]
     A[1:]=ac[1:n]
  S=np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(h)))
  r=np.arange(n)
  P=S[n-r]
  qbase=S[-1]-S[:n]
  p=P-A
  q=qbase-A
  # enforce p0/q0 maybe same
  p0=h.sum() - np.dot(h,h)
  p[0]=p0; q[0]=p0
  return p,q
```
Note for direct A: aut[:n][::-1] gives A0..A_{n-1}; good. For FFT A[1:]=ac[1:n] A1..; good.
Potential: `S[n-r]` indexing with array; S length n+1; for r=0 n, ok; r=n-1 1. Good.
`qbase=S[-1]-S[:n]` S[:n]=S_0..S_{n-1}; for r=0 total, good.

`compute_c5_count(h,n)`: p,q=compute_pq; return max(p.max(),q.max()) (float). Since p0/q0 included.

```
def enforce_sum(h,target):
   h=np.clip(h,0,1)
   diff=target-h.sum()
   if abs(diff)>1e-15:
      # choose interior
      interior=np.where((h>1e-12)&(h<1-1e-12))[0]
      if interior.size:
         # distribute diff over interior weighted room? We can adjust one with enough room
         if diff>0:
            order=np.argsort(1-h)
         else: order=np.argsort(h)
         for idx in order:
            room = (1-h[idx]) if diff>0 else h[idx]
            adj = min(room, abs(diff))
            h[idx] += np.sign(diff)*adj
            diff -= np.sign(diff)*adj
            if abs(diff)<1e-15: break
   return h
```
Our `project_feasible` already does proportional, may be enough. But final loops can ensure. Use top-level `enforce_sum`.

```
def softmax(y):
  y=np.asarray(y,float)
  y=y-y.max()
  e=np.exp(y)
  s=e.sum()
  if s<=0: e=np.ones_like(y)/len(y)

```

`project_feasible`:
```
def project_feasible(h, target):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    target = float(target)
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    for _ in range(200):
        diff = target - h.sum()
        if abs(diff) < 1e-14: break
        if diff > 0:
            room = 1.0 - h
            s = room.sum()
            if s < 1e-15: break
            h += (diff / s) * room
        else:
            s = h.sum()
            if s < 1e-15: break
            h += (diff / s) * h
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    h = enforce_sum(h, target)
    return h
```
But `enforce_sum` defined after? Python resolves at call time; can define before. Define enforce_sum first.
```
def enforce_sum(h,target):
   ...
   return h
```
In enforce, if diff positive and no room? unfeasible; clip. Use `if diff > 0` room. For interior with enough room? If not, loop.

`resample_h`:
```
def resample_h(h,n):
    if len(h)==n: return h.astype(float).copy()
    x0=np.linspace(0,2,len(h),endpoint=False)
    x1=np.linspace(0,2,n,endpoint=False)
    return np.interp(x1,x0,h)
```

`binarize`:
```
def binarize(h,target):
   h=np.asarray(h,float)
   n=len(h)
   target=float(target)
   hb=(h>=0.5).astype(float)
   diff=target-hb.sum()
   if abs(diff)>1e-10:
      if diff>0:
         zeros=np.where(hb<0.5)[0]
         order=np.argsort(-h[zeros])
         k=int(np.min((np.ceil(diff), len(zeros)))) if zeros.size else 0
         for idx in order[:k]: hb[idx]=1.0
      else:
         ones=np.where(hb>0.5)[0]
         order=np.argsort(h[ones])
         k=int(np.min((np.ceil(-diff), len(ones)))) if ones.size else 0
         for idx in order[:k]: hb[idx]=0.0
   # if still diff (target not integer), set one partial? For even not.
   diff=target-hb.sum()
   if abs(diff)>1e-10:
      if diff>0:
         z=np.where(hb<0.5)[0]
         if z.size: hb[z[0]]=min(1.0,hb[z[0]]+diff)
      else:
         o=np.where(hb>0.5)[0]
         if o.size: hb[o[0]]=max(0.0,hb[o[0]]+diff)
   return hb
```
For integer target, after threshold and flips sum exact (within). If parent fractional target .5, maybe not. We only use even.

```
def soft_c5_value(h,n,alpha):
   p,q=compute_pq(h)
   c=np.empty(2*n-1)
   c[:n]=p[::-1]; c[n:]=q[1:]
   val,_=soft_max_weights(c,alpha)
   return val
```

Now, `active_gradient`:
```
def active_gradient(typ,r,h,n):
   g=np.zeros(n)
   if typ==0:
      return 1.0 - 2.0*h
   m=n-r
   if m<=0: return g
   if typ==1:
      g[0:m] += 1.0 - h[r:]
      g[r:] -= h[0:m]
   else:
      g[r:] += 1.0 - h[0:m]
      g[0:m] -= h[r:]
   return g
```
For r=1,n=2,m=1 p: g[0]+=1-h1; g[1:]-=h0. Good.

`fractional_active_descent` implement. Need `best_c_count` variable. At end return exact C but if exact C slightly higher than best_count*dx, may return best_h with exact. We track best_h exact? We can track best_count and best_h; at end compute exact best_h. If exact due to sum projection maybe diff. Could also evaluate best_h after each improvement exact? Too expensive? We can use count. At end project best_h; exact.

Pseudo active descent final:
```
def fractional_active_descent(h,n,deadline):
    target=n/2
    h=project_feasible(h,target)
    p,q=compute_pq(h)
    cur=float(max(p.max(),q.max()))
    best_c=cur; best_h=h.copy()
    max_rounds=500
    for rd in range(max_rounds):
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       cmax=cur
       tol=max(1e-9,1e-5*cmax)
       types=[]; rs=[]; vals=[]
       if p[0] >= cmax - tol: types.append(0); rs.append(0); vals.append(float(p[0]))
       for r in range(1,n):
          if p[r] >= cmax - tol: types.append(1); rs.append(r); vals.append(float(p[r]))
          if q[r] >= cmax - tol: types.append(2); rs.append(r); vals.append(float(q[r]))
       if types:
          order=np.argsort(vals)[::-1][:8]
       else: order=np.array([],int)
       best_new=cur; best_pair=None
       for oi in order:
           typ=types[oi]; r=rs[oi]
           g=active_gradient(typ,r,h,n)
           pos=np.where(g > 1e-8)[0]
           neg=np.where(g < -1e-8)[0]
           if pos.size==0 or neg.size==0: continue
           pos=pos[np.argsort(-g[pos])[:12]]
           neg=neg[np.argsort(g[neg])[:12]]
           for i in pos:
              hi=h[i]
              if hi <= 1e-8: continue
              for j in neg:
                 hj=h[j]
                 if hj >= 1-1e-8: continue
                 if i==j: continue
                 lim=min(hi,1-hj)
                 if lim <=1e-8: continue
                 for frac in (0.05,0.15,0.3,0.5,0.8,1.0):
                    t=lim*frac
                    nm=transfer_new_max(h,p,q,int(i),int(j),t)
                    if nm < best_new - 1e-11:
                       best_new=nm; best_pair=(int(i),int(j),t)
                 if time.time()>deadline: break
              if time.time()>deadline: break
           if best_pair is not None and best_pair[2] < 1e-9: ...
       if best_pair is None:
           # gradient pair
           improved = gradient_pair_improve(h,p,q,n,50000,deadline)
           # after it, recompute p,q cur; if improved and cur<best update, else if not improved break
           if not improved:
               break
       else:
           apply_transfer_inplace(h,p,q,best_pair[0],best_pair[1],best_pair[2])
       p,q=compute_pq(h)
       cur=float(max(p.max(),q.max()))
       if cur < best_c - 1e-11:
           best_c=cur; best_h=h.copy()
       # if cur not lower for many? We only accept improvements in line search; so cur should lower when best_pair. If gradient_pair_improve accepts only improvement.
    best_h=project_feasible(best_h,n/2)
    return best_h, compute_c5_exact(best_h,n)
```

`lbfgs_refine` may call minimize 2-3 times per alpha. Let's implement:
```
def lbfgs_refine(h,n,deadline,alpha_list,best_h,best_c,mu=50):
    hcur=h.copy(); target=n/2; bounds=[(0,1)]*n
    for alpha in alpha_list:
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       stale=0
       for _ in range(3):
          if time.time()>=deadline: break
          try:
             res=minimize(lbfgs_obj,hcur,jac=lbfgs_grad,args=(n,alpha,mu),method='L-BFGS-B',bounds=bounds,options={'maxiter':200,...})
          except Exception: break
          hproj=project_feasible(res.x,target)
          c=compute_c5_exact(hproj,n)
          if c < best_c - 1e-13:
             best_c=c; best_h=hproj.copy(); stale=0
          else:
             stale+=1
          hcur=hproj
          if stale >=2: break
    return best_h,best_c
```
If exact not improve but soft improved? We still set hcur, maybe later exact improves? If stale after 2 break. Good.

Now, `run` phases in code. Need ensure `best_h` and `best_c` update correctly. Use helper? No closures. We'll manually.

Pseudo run:
```
def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0=time.time(); deadline=t0+budget_s-15
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    random.seed(seed)
    # parent
    try:
       h_parent=np.asarray(initial_h_values,float).copy()
    except Exception:
       h_parent=None
    if h_parent is not None and h_parent.size:
       nparent=len(h_parent)
    else:
       nparent=0
    if 100 <= nparent <= 1200 and nparent %2 ==0:
       n_use=nparent
       h_init=project_feasible(h_parent,n_use/2)
    else:
       n_use=500
       if h_parent is not None and h_parent.size:
          h_init=resample_h(h_parent,n_use)
       else:
          h_init=np.full(n_use,0.5)
       h_init=project_feasible(h_init,n_use/2)
    best_h=h_init.copy(); best_c=compute_c5_exact(h_init,n_use); best_n=n_use
    span=budget_s-15
    d1=t0+0.06*span
    # active parent
    if time.time()<d1:
       h_new,c_new=fractional_active_descent(best_h,best_n,d1)
       if c_new<best_c: best_c=c_new; best_h=h_new
    d2=t0+0.12*span
    if time.time()<d2:
       h4,c4=fourier_refine(best_n,d2,rng,best_h)
       if h4 is not None and c4<best_c: best_c=c4; best_h=h4
    d3=t0+0.28*span
    if time.time()<d3:
       h_l,c_l=lbfgs_refine(best_h,best_n,d3,[2000,10000,50000],best_h,best_c,50)
       if c_l<best_c: ...
       # maybe from parent if different
       if time.time()<d3:
          h_l2,c_l2=lbfgs_refine(h_init,best_n,d3,[20000,100000],h_init,compute_c5_exact(h_init,n_use),50) ...
    d4=t0+0.55*span
    if time.time()<d4 and n_use%2==0:
       starts=make_starts(n_use, h_init, rng)
       if best_h is not None: starts.insert(0,best_h)
       # evaluate and quick sort? compute exact C for each (maybe 15)
       scored=[]
       for h in starts: ...
       scored.sort? no lambda. Use Cvals array.
       top = ... top min(4)
       # allocate binary time among top
       num=len(top)
       slice=(d4-time.time())/num
       for idx,hstart in enumerate(top):
          phase_dead=min(d4, time.time()+max(5,slice))
          hb,cb=binary_local_search(hstart,n_use,phase_dead,rng)
          if cb<best_c: best_c=cb; best_h=hb
          # after binary, active polish if time remaining small? maybe if time<d4:
          if time.time()<d4 and cb<=best_c+1e-12:
             ha,ca=fractional_active_descent(hb,n_use,d4)
             if ca<best_c: best_c=ca; best_h=ha
    d5=t0+0.67*span
    if time.time()<d5:
       basis_cands=[best_h,h_init]
       # add some starts? We don't have starts ifPhase4 skipped. Could call make_quick_seeds? We can make a few random seeds now.
       if time.time() < d5-10:
          extra=make_starts(n_use, h_init, rng)
          basis_cands.extend(extra[:6])
       fb,fc=basis_refine(basis_cands,best_n,d5,best_h,best_c)
       if fc<best_c: ...
    d6=t0+0.82*span
    if time.time()<d6:
       h_l,c_l=lbfgs_refine(best_h,best_n,d6,[5000,20000,100000],best_h,best_c,50)
       ...
    d7=t0+0.94*span
    if time.time()<d7:
       h_a,c_a=fractional_active_descent(best_h,best_n,d7)
       if c_a<best_c: ...
    # final: if time left, maybe one more active with small deadline and exact
    if time.time()<deadline-2:
       h_a,c_a=fractional_active_descent(best_h,best_n,deadline-2)
       if c_a<best_c: ...
    best_h=project_feasible(best_h,best_n/2)
    best_c=compute_c5_exact(best_h,best_n)
    return best_h, float(best_c), int(best_n)
```

Potential bug in `enforce_sum`: If diff positive and interior room not enough due to all at 1? target feasible so sum below target implies room. But due to clip, maybe. Use loop over indices sorted by room.
```
def enforce_sum(h,target):
    h=np.asarray(h,float).copy()
    target=float(target)
    diff=target-h.sum()
    if abs(diff)<1e-15: return h
    if diff>0:
       idx=np.where(h<1.0)[0]
       order=np.argsort(1.0-h[idx]) if idx.size else np.array([],int)
    else:
       idx=np.where(h>0.0)[0]
       order=np.argsort(h[idx]) if idx.size else np.array([],int)
    for pos in order:
       k=int(pos)
       if diff>0:
          adj=min(1.0-h[k], diff)
          if adj<=1e-15: continue
          h[k]+=adj; diff-=adj
       else:
          adj=min(h[k], -diff)
          if adj<=1e-15: continue
          h[k]-=adj; diff+=adj
       if abs(diff)<1e-15: break
    return h
```
But if idx empty, order empty. `np.argsort(1.0-h[idx])`; if idx array. Good.

`project_feasible` calls enforce_sum after iterations. Good.

If target integer, diff integer. Use `k = int(np.minimum(np.ceil(abs(diff)), len(order)))`. Good. But if diff=0.5 due to rounding, maybe k=1. Fine.

```
def make_starts(n,parent,rng):
    starts=[]

       starts.append(project_feasible(parent,n/2))

    starts.append(random_balanced(n,rng))
    alphas=[0.6180339887,0.70710678,0.66666666,0.55555555]
    for a in alphas:
       starts.append(beatty_binary(n,a,float(rng.uniform(0,1)),rng))
    starts.append(wave_binary(n,1.0,0.0,rng))
    starts.append(wave_binary(n,2.0,1.0,rng))
    starts.append(wave_binary(n,3.0,2.0,rng))
    # maybe a couple random smooth
    starts.append(wave_binary(n,5.0,0.5,rng))
    return starts
```

`beatty_binary`: If n even target n//2. Use first m positions of a Beatty sequence? Low discrepancy. Need if unique less than m, pad. If unique greater? not. But if m=0. Use:
```
def beatty_binary(n,alpha,offset,rng):
    m=n//2
    h=np.zeros(n)
    if m<=0: return h
    base=(np.floor(np.arange(m)*alpha + offset).astype(int)) % n
    uniq=np.unique(base)
    h[uniq]=1.0
    if uniq.size < m:
       zeros=np.where(h==0)[0]
       add=rng.choice(zeros, size=min(m-uniq.size, len(zeros)), replace=False)
       h[add]=1.0
    if h.sum() > m:
       ones=np.where(h==1)[0]
       remove=ones[:int(h.sum()-m)]
       h[remove]=0
    return h
```
If alpha very small, collisions large; pad random. Good.

`wave_binary`: score = sin + 0.2*normal; `binarize(score, n/2)`. For frequency maybe f cycles over [0,2]. Use `2*pi*freq*x`. Good.


```
def basis_refine(cands,n,deadline,best_h,best_c,rng):
    B_list=[]
    for cc in cands:
       if cc is None: continue
       vv=np.asarray(cc,float)
       if len(vv)!=n: continue
       vv=project_feasible(vv,n/2)
       dup=False
       for uu in B_list:
          if np.max(np.abs(vv-uu)) < 1e-5:
             dup=True; break
       if not dup:
          B_list.append(vv)
          if len(B_list) >= 12: break
    if not B_list:
       return best_h,best_c
    B=np.array(B_list)
    K=B.shape[0]
    dx=2.0/n
    starts=[np.zeros(K)]
    for kk in range(min(K,5)):
       y=np.full(K,-8.0); y[kk]=8.0; starts.append(y)
    for _ in range(3):
       starts.append(rng.normal(size=K)*2.0)
    for y0 in starts:
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       try:
          res=minimize(basis_obj,y0,jac=basis_grad,args=(B,n,20000),method='L-BFGS-B',options={'maxiter':200,...})
       except Exception:
          res=None
       if res is not None: yres=res.x
       else: yres=y0
       a=softmax(yres)
       h=B.dot(a); h=project_feasible(h,n/2)
       c=compute_c5_exact(h,n)
       if c<best_c: best_c=c; best_h=h.copy()
       # coordinate descent on a
       cur=c
       alpha=a.copy()
       hcur=h.copy()
       for rd in range(60):
          if time.time()>deadline: break
          improved=False
          for s in range(K):
             if improved: break
             if alpha[s] <= 1e-8: continue
             for t in range(K):
                if improved: break
                if s==t: continue
                for frac in (0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0):
                   delta=alpha[s]*frac
                   a2=alpha.copy()
                   a2[s]-=delta; a2[t]+=delta
                   if a2[s] < -1e-12 or a2[t] < -1e-12: continue
                   a2=np.clip(a2,0.0,1.0)
                   ss=a2.sum()
                   if ss<=0: continue
                   a2/=ss
                   h2=B.dot(a2)
                   c2=compute_c5_count(h2,n)*dx
                   if c2 < cur - 1e-13:
                      alpha=a2; hcur=h2; cur=c2; improved=True; break
          if improved and cur < best_c:
             best_c=cur; best_h=hcopy? Need exact maybe count vs exact. Use exact at end of coordinate descent or when update: c_exact=compute_c5_exact(hcur,n); if < best. To avoid too many exact, use count for local and exact when cur < best_c? best_c exact; count*dx close. We can set best_h=hcur and best_c=cur (C approx). At very end exact. Here okay.
          if not improved: break
       hfin=project_feasible(hcur,n/2); cfin=compute_c5_exact(hfin,n)
       if cfin < best_c: best_c=cfin; best_h=hfin
    return best_h,best_c
```
